- **DEC-621 — Uplift actions run Phase 1's `apply_actions` on a copy and graft its output columns onto the original frame.** *Context:* plan B §5 keeps the control-group fraction and the suppression rules unchanged. Copying them into the uplift package would create two implementations that drift. *Decision:* `apply_uplift_actions` puts the uplift into the `actions.score_field` column of a copy, calls `engine.stages.actions.apply_actions` on it unchanged, and copies back only `band`, `action`, `suppressed_reason`, `control_group` and the attrs that list applied and skipped suppression rules. Band and action are then overwritten by segment. The input frame's own `score_field` column, if any, is returned untouched. *Consequences:* the same run id holds out the same customers under both problem types, and a suppression change reaches uplift runs without a second edit.
- **DEC-622 — When the budget and the cost cut end at the same row, the stop reason is `value_below_cost`.** *Context:* `stop_reason` answers "why is N not larger?". *Decision:* if both limits bind at the same row, a bigger budget would not change N, so the cost is the real limit. A cost cut that removes even the first persuadable gives N = 0 with `value_below_cost`; no eligible persuadable gives `no_persuadables`. *Consequences:* the reason shown points to the setting that would change the answer.
- **DEC-623 — Sleeping dogs are excluded by segment, not by an uplift threshold, and the rule is checked twice.** *Context:* plan B says sleeping dogs are never treated, whatever the configuration. *Decision:* `choose_contacts` only ever picks rows labelled persuadable, so no budget, cost or value can choose a sleeping dog. It raises `RuntimeError` if one is ever selected, and `apply_uplift_actions` checks again that no sleeping dog is `Treat` or `intended_treatment` before it returns. *Consequences:* the checks cannot fire by construction, but the rule matters enough to fail loudly rather than export.
- **DEC-624 — `intended_treatment` marks the selected rows plus the control rows the policy would have selected, and is empty when nobody is selected.** *Context:* a campaign is measured by comparing treated with control customers. Comparing treated persuadables with a control group drawn from every segment would mix the campaign's effect with the difference between segments. *Decision:* the column marks the selected rows and the held-out rows that pass the same selection rule. How "would have been selected" is decided at a tie is DEC-606. With nobody selected, nobody is intended. `measure_incrementality` compares treated with control inside this set on an uplift run. *Consequences:* both arms pass the same rule and the control side is a random draw, which is what makes the comparison unbiased.
- **DEC-625 — The sure-thing cut defaults to the training base rate, and records that it did.** *Context:* plan B §6 gives no default for the cut between sure things and lost causes. *Decision:* when `sure_thing_min_probability` is unset, the training base rate is used and `SegmentThresholds.sure_thing_from_base_rate` is `True`. The thresholds go on the model card, and scoring runs replay them rather than recompute a base rate from the rows being scored. *Consequences:* a customer's segment does not depend on which other customers are in the scoring file.
- **DEC-626 — Missing or non-finite predictions are refused, not given a segment.** *Context:* a default segment for a row the model could not score would be an invented recommendation. *Decision:* segments, policy and actions raise `ValueError` with counts only, never values, as `engine/stages/actions.py` does. *Consequences:* a broken prediction stops the run instead of reaching a treat list.
- **DEC-627 — Every 95% band in uplift uses the exact normal quantile.** *Context:* incrementality and OPE each need a z value, and 1.96 is a rounding. *Decision:* one `Z_95 = NormalDist().inv_cdf(0.975)` is shared by the Newcombe interval and the OPE intervals. It reproduces Newcombe 1998 Table II to four decimals. *Consequences:* the two modules cannot drift apart on one constant.
- **DEC-628 — An undefined p-value is null.** *Context:* when both arms are all 0 or all 1 the pooled z statistic is 0/0. *Decision:* `incrementality_report.json` writes `null` and the UI shows "—", instead of the conventional 1.0. *Consequences:* the no-fabricated-numbers rule holds for the p-value too.
- **DEC-629 — Campaign rows without a usable outcome are counted, never imputed.** *Context:* treating a missing outcome as a non-conversion would bias the lift towards zero. *Decision:* a row with no match in the outcomes file, a null outcome or an unreadable treatment date counts towards `rows_without_outcome`; immature rows are counted separately in `rows_immature`. Neither is filled in. *Consequences:* the report says how many customers it could not measure and why.
- **DEC-630 — Campaign-results inputs are read tolerantly, and duplicate keys are refused.** *Context:* the scores may come from `scores.csv`, whose flags are text, and keys may be typed differently in two files. *Decision:* `control_group`, `intended_treatment` and `suppressed_reason` are read from bool, 0/1 or text, a blank `suppressed_reason` means not suppressed, and primary keys are compared as trimmed text so an integer key matches a string key. Duplicate keys in either file raise `ValueError` (a 422 from the API), because they would double-count outcomes. *Consequences:* the same report comes from `scores.parquet` or a round-tripped CSV.
- **DEC-631 — A campaign report is causal exactly when the engine drew a non-empty control group among the eligible rows.** *Context:* the report compares treated with control; that is an experiment only if the control group was random. *Decision:* the engine draws the holdout by a per-row hash, which is random by design, so `causal` is true exactly when that holdout is non-empty among eligible rows. *Consequences:* the flag is a fact about the run, not a setting.
- **DEC-632 — Off-policy evaluation validates its inputs strictly.** *Context:* IPS and DR divide by the logging propensity. *Decision:* logging propensities must be strictly inside (0, 1): a row that could only get one action says nothing about the other, and its weight would be infinite. `t` and `y` must be 0/1, the policy and outcome predictions must be probabilities (1e-9 slack, then clipped), and all arrays must have one length. Anything else is `ValueError` (422 `OPE_INVALID`). A `top_share` rule counts rows with `metrics.top_rows`, so "the top 7%" is the same rows in OPE as in uplift@7%. *Consequences:* an estimate is never produced from inputs that make it meaningless.
- **DEC-633 — The uplift feature spec reuses Phase 1's validation verdicts.** *Context:* Phase 1 validation already decides which columns are mostly empty, constant, ID-like or personal data, and the user can acknowledge those findings. *Decision:* `fit_feature_spec` drops a column when its `HIGH_NULL_COLUMN`, `CONSTANT_COLUMN` or `HIGH_CARDINALITY_ID_LIKE` finding says `will_be_dropped` and was not acknowledged, and drops `PII_DETECTED` columns unless `pii_handling` is `keep`. Its own constant and ID-like tests, using Phase 1's `ID_DISTINCT_RATIO` and `ID_LIKE_PATTERN`, only cover columns Phase 1 did not rule on. *Consequences:* the Validate page and the uplift features cannot disagree.
- **DEC-634 — Dates are never uplift features.** *Context:* in an uplift table a date is almost always about the campaign (sent, opened, converted) and leaks the treatment or the outcome. *Decision:* date-like columns (datetime dtype, or text Phase 1's `infer_column_type` reads as a date; numbers are never dates) are dropped with reason `date`, and `FEATURE_AFTER_TREATMENT` inspects exactly those columns. *Consequences:* a date that genuinely describes the customer must be turned into a number (such as tenure) before upload.
- **DEC-635 — Categorical levels are frozen at fit time.** *Context:* LightGBM codes categories by position, so a new level at scoring time could shift every other level's code. *Decision:* the 100 most frequent levels (ties broken by level text) are kept as pandas `category` columns with fixed categories. At scoring time an unseen level becomes missing. *Consequences:* a scoring file cannot silently change what the model's splits mean.
- **DEC-636 — `TREATMENT_NOT_RANDOM` is a 3-fold out-of-fold AUC of a small deterministic LightGBM.** *Context:* plan B §4 asks for "a quick classifier" with an AUC limit of 0.60. *Decision:* 100 trees, 15 leaves, `min_child_samples` 50, `deterministic=True`, `n_jobs=2`, a seeded `StratifiedKFold`, on the same features the learners use, over at most 50,000 rows sampled in proportion from each arm. The AUC is about 0.5 under random assignment and about 0.88–0.90 on the test generator's targeted design, well clear of the limit. The message names only features carrying at least 10% of the split gain. Acknowledging `TREATMENT_NOT_RANDOM` or `TREATMENT_NOT_RANDOM:<treatment column>` lets the run pass with `causal: false`. *Consequences:* an error inside the fit is raised, not swallowed, because a silently skipped check would let a targeted campaign be called causal.
- **DEC-637 — Rows whose outcome cannot be shown to be mature are excluded, never guessed.** *Context:* `OUTCOME_WINDOW_IMMATURE` says some outcomes may still change. *Decision:* rows whose treatment date plus the window is after now, and rows with no readable treatment date, are removed through the check's `keep` mask and counted. Arm sizes are counted on the kept rows, so `TREATMENT_ARM_TOO_SMALL` reflects the data that will be trained on. *Consequences:* a model is never trained on a not-yet-converted customer labelled as a non-converter.
- **DEC-638 — A configured treatment column is never replaced by a hint.** *Context:* `treatment_column_hints` helps when nothing is configured. *Decision:* if the user named the column that records the experiment and it is missing, the answer is `TREATMENT_COLUMN_MISSING`, not a silently measured different column. Hints are used only when nothing is configured. *Consequences:* the run always measures the experiment the user meant.
- **DEC-639 — The uplift module adds its own entry links to Phase 1 screens after they are drawn.** *Context:* no Phase 1 file may be edited, yet users need a way in. *Decision:* `ui/modules/uplift/index.js` watches `#app`'s children with a `MutationObserver`. On the Overview it inserts one `<nav class="uentry">` linking to `#/uplift`; on any `#/uc/<id>` screen it links to `#/uplift/<id>`; on a Phase 1 run whose `GET /runs/{id}` reports `mode: score` (asked once per run) it also links to `#/campaign/<id>/<run>`. The link text never repeats the use-case name, so Phase 1 selectors that find a link by that name still match one link. *Consequences:* removing the module removes the links; Phase 1 markup is untouched.
- **DEC-640 — The UI claims two route prefixes, `uplift` and `campaign`.** *Context:* `app.js` asks `resolveRoute` before its own `#/uc/...` routing, so claiming `uc` would take over Phase 1 screens. *Decision:* everything after the two prefixes belongs to the module, as with Phase 3a's single `generative` prefix. *Consequences:* Phase 1 URLs are unaffected.
- **DEC-641 — The module repairs deep links to its own URLs.** *Context:* `index.html` loads module scripts after `app.js`, whose first render runs before the module has registered and would draw the Overview. *Decision:* when the module loads it renders the uplift screen if the URL is one of its own; if the Overview is drawn over it afterwards, the observer draws the uplift screen again, at most 3 times per navigation, and a per-render counter stops a late render from drawing over the current screen. *Consequences:* a bookmarked uplift URL opens the right screen.
- **DEC-642 — Rendering is pure; fetching lives in `controller.js`.** *Context:* the screens need tests without a browser, jsdom or `npm install`. *Decision:* `views.js`, `charts.js` and `format.js` render from artefact objects only; `controller.js` fetches. The node suites render from artefact fixtures, and the Python test runs them and skips them, printing the reason, when `node` is missing. *Consequences:* every screen is testable from JSON alone.
- **DEC-643 — Charts are inline SVG drawn with the theme's colour variables.** *Context:* the Model and Output pages need a Qini curve, decile bars that can go below zero and a four-segment chart, in both themes. *Decision:* inline SVG using `index.html`'s variables (`--c`, `--t`, `--bad`, `--faint`): the Qini curve with a dashed random line and the area between them shaded; uplift by decile as bars (negative ones red) with a dot for predicted uplift; the segments as bars sized by share. A decile with no observed uplift gets no bar and an em dash. *Consequences:* no new palette and no chart library.
- **DEC-644 — A "not causal" banner appears whenever any artefact on a screen has `causal: false`, and a missing value is always "—".** *Context:* plan B §8. *Decision:* the banner text mirrors `engine.uplift.contracts.NOT_CAUSAL_NOTE`, and a test keeps the two identical. Expected incremental conversions, AUUC and lift always show their interval, or "CI —" when there is none. *Consequences:* a point estimate never looks more certain than it is.
- **DEC-645 — An immature campaign report shows only "Results available on \<date\>".** *Context:* plan B §8. *Decision:* no lift or rate is shown while the report is immature. `results_available_on` is a plain date, so it is formatted in UTC (read as local time it would show the previous day west of Greenwich). A mature report that still excluded immature rows shows the excluded count and the date every row matures. *Consequences:* nobody reads an early, biased lift off the page.
- **DEC-646 — Uplift runs reuse Phase 1's flows by subclassing, dispatched by one lookup.** *Context:* plan B §5 asks for a one-line registry lookup and no change to the classification path. *Decision:* `UpliftTrainFlow` and `UpliftScoreFlow` subclass `engine.pipeline._TrainFlow` and `_ScoreFlow` and replace only the stage bodies, so status, run record, manifest, cancel and failure handling are the code Phase 1 shipped. `Pipeline.run_train` and `run_score` each gained one line asking `uplift_flow_for` (in the pipeline's Phase 3b block). It returns `None` for a context without a `UseCaseConfig`, a non-uplift training config, or a score run whose model is not AUUC. A score run is uplift when its version (the one named, else the champion) has metric AUUC or its config says uplift. *Consequences:* Phase 1 behaviour is unchanged, and the Running screen shows the same rows for both.
- **DEC-647 — Uplift training writes no `prepare.json`; the feature spec lives on the model card.** *Context:* `PrepareReport`'s drop reasons cannot say "date" or "unsupported type", and its transforms cannot describe fixed category levels, without mislabelling them. *Decision:* `model/uplift_model.json` records the feature columns, the levels and every dropped column with its real reason (the additive, defaulted field `UpliftModelCard.dropped_columns`). `split.json` is written honestly, with a zero-row validation part. *Consequences:* Phase 1's Data page shows no prepare report for an uplift run.
- **DEC-648 — `schema.json` describes the raw training columns, so Phase 1's scoring validation works unchanged.** *Context:* scoring an uplift model goes through Phase 1's `POST /runs` and `validate_against_schema`, which types the uploaded file as uploaded. *Decision:* the schema describes the raw training columns of the fitted features, not the prepared category/float matrix. *Consequences:* no new scoring endpoint is needed. No drift baseline is written for uplift models.
- **DEC-649 — An uplift model never silently replaces a champion of another metric.** *Context:* the registry has one champion per use case, and AUUC cannot be compared with ROC AUC. *Decision:* when the champion is a propensity model the uplift challenger stays a candidate with the reason recorded, mirroring Phase 1's `METRIC_MISMATCH`. When it is an uplift model it is reloaded with its own feature spec, re-evaluated on this run's hold-out through `evaluate_uplift`, and `decide_uplift_champion` decides. `governance.approval_required` is honoured as in Phase 1 (`pending_approval` with `measured_against_champion_id`). DEC-609 covers the empty slot. *Consequences:* a person decides whether a use case's champion changes kind.
- **DEC-650 — The hold-out predictions are kept as `uplift_holdout.parquet`.** *Context:* off-policy evaluation and every later "expected incremental conversions" need the hold-out's `t`, `y` and out-of-sample predictions. *Decision:* one file per training run (`primary_key`, `t`, `y`, `uplift`, `p_treated`, `p_control`). A scoring run's recommendation reads its model's training hold-out and bootstraps it with the training run's seed, so its interval matches the training recommendation's. *Consequences:* OPE can run any time after training without refitting.
- **DEC-651 — Score runs of uplift models are recorded as `problem_type: uplift`.** *Context:* they are created through Phase 1's `POST /runs`, which records the use case's own problem type. *Decision:* once the uplift score flow has resolved an AUUC version, it rewrites `run.json` with `problem_type: uplift` and `headline_metric: auuc`. *Consequences:* run lists and the uplift UI filter scoring runs correctly.
- **DEC-652 — The uplift `ScoringSummary` is filled from Phase 1's own summariser.** *Context:* Phase 1's Output and runs list read `scoring_summary.json`. *Decision:* action, suppression, control-group and sample-row counts come from `export.summarise` on a config copy whose score field is `uplift`. The bands are then replaced by the four segments and the KPI by "Persuadables recommended to contact" (the policy's `contacts_recommended`). *Consequences:* nothing in the summary is estimated.
- **DEC-653 — The randomness check is seeded from the upload id, in the API and in the run.** *Context:* `POST /uplift/runs` and the run's validate stage both run `TREATMENT_NOT_RANDOM`. Seeded from the run id, a borderline file the request admitted could be blocked by the run. *Decision:* both use `seed_from(upload_id)` (`engine.uplift.flow.check_seed`). *Consequences:* the synchronous verdict and the in-run verdict agree on the same file.
- **DEC-654 — `POST /uplift/runs` answers a blocked request with both reports at the top level.** *Context:* the Setup screen renders Phase 1's and the uplift findings from one response. *Decision:* the 409 body is `{detail, validation, uplift_validation}`, with `detail.code` `VALIDATION_FAILED` when Phase 1's checks block, else `UPLIFT_VALIDATION_FAILED`, in the same top-level shape as Phase 1's `POST /runs` 409 (DEC-058). Acknowledging is a resend with `overrides.validation.acknowledged`. *Consequences:* one parser serves both routes; the UI reads the reports from the top level first.
- **DEC-655 — `lightgbm` is pinned directly in `pyproject.toml`'s Phase 3b block.** *Context:* `engine/uplift` imports LightGBM directly (meta-learners and the randomness check); it was only a transitive dependency of AutoGluon's extra. *Decision:* pin `lightgbm==4.7.0`, the version already in `requirements-freeze.txt`. *Consequences:* no shared pin moves, and the freeze file is unchanged.
- **DEC-656 — The Criteo use case is written for the uplift problem type but stays uninstalled and planned.** *Context:* DEC-406 kept Criteo out for two reasons: the engine could not answer an uplift question, and the data could not be fetched here. Phase 3b removes the first. On 2026-09-23 `fetch.py` and `curl` were still refused (403) for `go.criteo.net`, `huggingface.co` and `ailab.criteo.com`. *Decision:* `library/criteo-uplift/use_case.yaml` now sets `problem_type: uplift`, `uplift.treatment_column: treatment`, outcome `conversion`, X-learner on LightGBM, `exposure` and `visit` excluded, and it resolves with the engine's config loader. It is not copied into `library/configs/use_cases/`, `ad_tech.yaml` keeps it `planned`, the library tests keep pinning that, and the non-commercial licence warning stays. *Consequences:* plan B's Criteo acceptance criterion is not met in this environment, and the run report says so rather than substituting synthetic numbers.
- **DEC-657 — Phase 5's hooks are the existing uplift code, not new code.** *Context:* plan B §12 asks for an agent-editable map and an `OUTCOMES_IMMATURE` rule for Phase 5's action-policy evaluator. *Decision:* the map is `engine.uplift.config.uplift_agent_editable_paths()`: `uplift.segments.*` and `uplift.policy.*` are editable, and every other uplift leaf is not. `OUTCOMES_IMMATURE` is represented by `IncrementalityStatus.IMMATURE` in `incrementality_report.json`, with `results_available_on`. OPE (`engine/uplift/ope.py`) is the evaluator. Both are announced in `docs/CROSS_BRANCH_REQUESTS.md`. *Consequences:* Phase 5 reads these names instead of Phase 3b guessing at an interface it has not seen.
- **DEC-658 — A date picked on the Campaign results page means the end of that day where the user is.** *Context:* the page's "as of" field is a plain date, and `POST /runs/{run_id}/campaign-results` needs an aware datetime. The first real-browser run sent `<date>T23:59:59Z`, so in India a user who picked 1 June read the result back as "as of 02 Jun, 5:29 am", because stamps are shown in local time. *Decision:* `controller.js`'s `endOfLocalDay` sends 23:59:59 of the picked day in the browser's time zone, as its UTC instant; an unreadable date falls back to the old UTC form. `results_available_on` stays a plain date formatted in UTC (DEC-645). *Consequences:* the day the user picked is the day the page reads back, in every time zone; the browser test runs in India's zone to keep it so (DEC-660).
- **DEC-659 — The uplift screens ask only for the artefacts a run's record lists, plus the two written on request.** *Context:* the Model and Output pages fetched every uplift artefact name. A scoring run writes no `uplift_validation.json`, so every Output visit logged a `404` as a console error, although the screen already showed "—". *Decision:* `run.json`'s `artefacts` map lists every file the run's own flow wrote; the screens request the names in it, plus `ope_report.json` and `incrementality_report.json`, which `POST .../uplift/ope` and `POST .../campaign-results` write after the run finished and which the record never lists. A record without the map (an older run) gets every name asked for, as before. *Consequences:* no request is made that the contract already knows will fail; a missing artefact still renders as "—".
- **DEC-660 — The uplift UI has a real-browser journey test, marked slow and skipped with its reason when no browser is available.** *Context:* the node suites render every screen from JSON and the API tests drive the routes in process; neither had ever opened a page. *Decision:* `tests/integration/uplift/test_uplift_browser.py` starts the real app under uvicorn on an isolated data directory and a copy of `configs/` (LightGBM and 30 resamples instead of AutoGluon and 200, the only difference from a user's run), then walks plan B §8 and §11 in Chromium through the markup only: entry link, upload, training, Model and Output pages checked number for number against the artefacts, scoring, Campaign results before and after maturity, the not-random refusal and acknowledgement, and a 390 px width. The console must stay clean apart from the `404`s the contract defines and the one `409` provoked on purpose. It runs in India's time zone (DEC-658). It is `@pytest.mark.slow` and skips, printing why, without playwright or a usable Chromium. *Consequences:* `make uplift-test` covers the screens end to end where a browser exists; `make test` does not pay for it.
- **DEC-661 — SNIPS is left out of the OPE report when it is undefined.** *Context:* SNIPS divides by `Σ w`. When no logged row took the action the policy would take, `Σ w = 0` and the estimate is 0/0; the engine reported 0.0 (the conformance track's one bug). `OpeEstimate.value` cannot be null. *Decision:* `_snips` returns `None` and `evaluate_policy` omits SNIPS from `estimates`; IPS and DR always appear, in that order. `OpeReport.estimates`' description and `docs/UPLIFT.md` §10 say so. *Consequences:* no invented number; a reader of `estimates` must look SNIPS up by `method`, not by position.
- **DEC-662 — `policy_from_rule` refuses `top_share = 0` and, given both rules, treats a row only when it passes both.** *Context:* the OPE request takes `top_share`, `min_uplift` or both, and the interface does not say how they combine or what a zero share means. *Decision:* `top_share` must be in (0, 1]: a policy that treats nobody by construction has nothing to evaluate, and 0 is far more often a unit slip than a request. With both rules the policy is their AND: the top share, *and only those* at or above the minimum uplift, as the description in words says. At least one rule is required. The top count is `metrics.top_rows` (exact ceiling, DEC-604), so `0.07·100` is 7 rows here as in uplift@7%. *Consequences:* a rule is always a subset of the top share the user named, never wider.
- **DEC-663 — A recommendation of nobody reports 0 incremental conversions with a [0, 0] band, not null.** *Context:* `recommend_policy` can end at N = 0 (no eligible persuadable, or a cost that removes the first one; DEC-622). A null there would read as "could not be measured". *Decision:* contacting nobody changes exactly nobody, so `expected_incremental_conversions` is 0 with `ci_low = ci_high = 0`. *Consequences:* the Output page shows "0" with its band rather than "—"; null keeps meaning "not measurable" (DEC-611).
- **DEC-664 — On a hold-out of under ten rows, empty deciles are left out.** *Context:* `np.array_split` of n < 10 ranked rows leaves some of the ten groups empty; their mean predicted uplift is undefined, but `UpliftDecile.predicted_uplift` is a non-optional float. *Decision:* `decile_table` returns only the non-empty groups, still numbered from 1 in ranking order, and `UpliftEvaluation.deciles`' description says "ten rows … fewer only on a hold-out of under ten rows". The oracle accepts this variant. *Consequences:* no number is invented for an empty group; any real hold-out (the arm floors ask for thousands) has ten.
- **DEC-665 — The independent oracle fixtures live in `tests/fixtures/uplift_oracle/` and run in the fast suite.** *Context:* the conformance track checked the engine against reference implementations written without reading it (metrics with ties, Newcombe's published examples, exact OPE cases, the X-learner's weighting direction, the policy rules, the champion cases). Kept only in a scratch directory, those checks would not survive the session. *Decision:* the small known-answer cases are JSON files under `tests/fixtures/uplift_oracle/` with their provenance, and `tests/unit/uplift/test_oracle_conformance.py` replays them in `make test`. The fixture builders stay outside the repository. The library cross-checks against scikit-uplift and causalml live in `tests/unit/uplift/test_metrics.py` behind `pytest.importorskip`, because neither library is a dependency: they run wherever the libraries are installed and skip with a reason elsewhere. Section 16 of `docs/UPLIFT.md` records the conversions between their conventions and the engine's. *Consequences:* a change that moves any of those answers fails the fast suite.
- **DEC-666 — The prototype follows DEC-608 and does not draw the Overview's uplift link.** *Context:* the reference prototype offered Uplift in Win-back's own Setup, as plan B §8 literally says, where the product does not (DEC-608). *Decision:* uplift starts on its own screen, `#/uplift/win-back-campaign`, reached from the use case's **Uplift for this use case ›** link, and Win-back's Phase 1 Setup is 8f0d358's again. The product's Overview link **Uplift modelling ›** (DEC-639) is *not* drawn, so screenshot 01 and the Data / Model / Output pages in 14 and 17 stay byte for byte 8f0d358's. *Consequences:* in the prototype `#/uplift` is reachable by URL only. Whether screenshots 01, 14 and 17 may change to carry the link is a reviewer's ruling, asked in `docs/CROSS_BRANCH_REQUESTS.md`.
- **DEC-667 — In the prototype an uplift run on Win-back is a candidate, not an "Uplift champion".** *Context:* the prototype labelled a causal uplift run **Uplift champion**, a label older than DEC-609 and DEC-649. Win-back is configured for classification and its slot holds a propensity champion, so the product keeps that run a candidate. *Decision:* the Model page's AUUC card reads **Candidate** with the reason (AUUC cannot be compared with ROC AUC; promote it on the Models page), the results line *Candidate · promote it on the Models page*, and no run carries an uplift-champion badge. Screenshots 21 were re-shot; the other uplift shots did not change. *Consequences:* the prototype and the product say the same thing about who holds the slot.
- **DEC-668 — Multi-treatment is added beside the binary fields, never instead of them.** *Context:* plan B §1.1 excludes several offers from this phase but asks for contracts that can take them. The review found nothing that blocks it: no `Literal` or `bool` encodes "two arms"; `causal` and `computed_on` are orthogonal to the number of arms. *Decision:* the extension path is additive. (1) `UpliftConfig` gains `treatment_levels` (the control value first) and the checks a per-configuration switch: `TREATMENT_NOT_BINARY` stays the rule when one level is configured, and becomes "a value outside the configured levels" otherwise. (2) The artefacts gain optional per-arm structures - `arms: tuple[ArmSummary, ...]` on the evaluation, segments, policy and incrementality reports, `uplift_by_arm` on `UpliftPrediction` and the hold-out file - each arm compared with the control. (3) Every existing field keeps its meaning as the first treatment against control: `treated_rows`, `control_rows`, `treated_rate`, `p_treated`/`p_control`, `uplift`, `intended_treatment`. (4) The champion rule and the policy choose per arm only once a DEC says how arms are compared (for example the best arm per customer); until then a multi-arm run is refused. *Consequences:* a reader written for this phase keeps working on a multi-treatment artefact; `schema_version` moves only when a field changes meaning, which this path never does.
- **DEC-669 — Plan B's `evaluate_uplift.py` is `engine/uplift/metrics.py::evaluate_uplift`.** *Context:* plan B §5 says "evaluate_uplift.py is new; evaluate.py untouched except a one-line registry lookup". *Decision:* the uplift evaluator is `evaluate_uplift` in `engine/uplift/metrics.py`, beside the metric definitions it applies, and `UpliftTrainFlow._evaluate` calls it. `engine/stages/evaluate.py` is untouched (no line at all); the one-line lookup the plan allows lives in `Pipeline.run_train` and `run_score` instead (DEC-646). A module that only re-exported the function would add a second name for one thing. *Consequences:* `git diff 8f0d358 -- engine/stages/evaluate.py` stays empty.
- **DEC-670 — `uplift_evaluation.json` carries a hold-out fingerprint, and the champion rule compares it.** *Context:* the champion rule refused evaluations with different row, treated or control counts, but two different hold-outs of the same size passed (the critic's case). The flow re-scores the champion on this run's rows, so it was safe in practice; a caller comparing two stored evaluations was not. *Decision:* `UpliftEvaluation.holdout_fingerprint` (optional, additive) is the sha256 of the hold-out's primary keys as text, sorted and newline-joined (`engine.uplift.metrics.holdout_digest`); `evaluate_uplift(..., holdout_keys=)` sets it, and both of the flow's calls pass the keys. `decide_uplift_champion` raises `ValueError` when both evaluations carry a fingerprint and they differ; one without a fingerprint (written before this field) falls back to the counts. *Consequences:* "same hold-out" is checked on the customers, not only on their number.
- **DEC-671 — `uplift.learner` accepts plan B's `s`, `t` and `x`.** *Context:* plan B §6 writes `learner (s|t|x, default x)`; the enum's values are `s_learner`, `t_learner`, `x_learner`, and `learner: x` was refused. *Decision:* a `mode="before"` validator maps `s`, `t` and `x` (any case, trimmed) to the full names; the stored and reported value is always the full name, and the JSON schema keeps the three full names. *Consequences:* a use case or run override written from the plan loads; nothing downstream ever sees a short form.
- **DEC-672 — The branch is `claude/funny-mendel-faui36`; the protocol and the requests call it `phase-3b-uplift`.** *Context:* `PARALLEL_WORK_PROTOCOL.md` §1 and every `CROSS_BRANCH_REQUESTS` entry name the branch `phase-3b-uplift`, as plan B does; the work was done on a branch the session harness named. *Decision:* `phase-3b-uplift` stays the workstream's name in the protocol, the requests and the shared-file markers, because that is what other branches look for; the physical branch is recorded here. When it merges it can be renamed or merged under either name. *Consequences:* nothing to change in any other file; a reader who looks for `phase-3b-uplift` on the remote is told where the work is.
- **DEC-673 — `docs/UPLIFT.md` §3 is the normative table of the six uplift check codes until `docs/DATA_CONTRACT.md` lists them.** *Context:* plan B §4 says "add to the Phase 1 check registry", and plan §13.4 makes the validation table in `docs/DATA_CONTRACT.md` the home of every user-facing code. The registry half is DEC-603. `DATA_CONTRACT.md` is not a Phase 3b file and has no Phase 3b block. *Decision:* `docs/UPLIFT.md` §3 lists each code with its severity, meaning, literal message and suggestion, and is the reference meanwhile; a `CROSS_BRANCH_REQUESTS` entry asks the trunk owner to add or link the six codes in `DATA_CONTRACT.md`. *Consequences:* every uplift finding is documented once, in words a reviewer can check against `engine/uplift/checks.py`.
- **DEC-674 — "Sleeping dogs are never treated" is guaranteed on the model's segments; on the true segments it is bounded, not absolute.** *Context:* plan B §9 says "sleeping dogs never recommended" on planted data. By construction no row the *model* calls a sleeping dog is ever treated (DEC-623). A true sleeping dog the model misjudges as persuadable can be: the audit's run of the API path (10,000 training rows, LightGBM, persuadable cut 0.05, 8,000 scored) treated 20 of 983 true sleeping dogs, and the test's pinned run 26 of 983. No estimated model can promise zero on the truth. *Decision:* the guarantee is on predicted segments, checked twice in code; on the true segments the integration test `test_true_sleeping_dogs_are_rarely_treated_and_far_below_their_share` asserts at most 5% of true sleeping dogs are treated and that their share among the treated is at most a quarter of their share in the population. Raising the default persuadable cut or adding a margin rule is a product decision this entry does not take. *Consequences:* the claim the product makes is one it can keep; a model that stops separating sleeping dogs fails the test.
- **DEC-675 — `qini_curve.json` carries `causal`, as a required field.** *Context:* every uplift artefact carries the flag so any screen can show the not-causal banner; `QiniCurve` did not, and a test hid it with `hasattr`. *Decision:* `QiniCurve.causal: bool` has no default - a default of true would call an unknown curve causal - and `evaluate_uplift` sets it from the run. The Model page's banner reads it too, and the integration test checks the flag on every artefact without the guard. *Consequences:* a `qini_curve.json` written before this field no longer validates as a model; none exists outside tests, and the route serves the stored bytes unchanged.
- **DEC-676 — The surrogate's fidelity is labelled in-sample wherever it is shown, and is reproducible for the same rows.** *Context:* DEC-616 records that `explanation_fidelity` is the surrogate's in-sample R² for the S- and T-learners and AutoGluon, but the feature-importance caption and the Running line said only "R² 0.86". *Decision:* both now say "in-sample R²". The surrogate is fitted deterministically, so the same rows give the same fidelity and the same contributions every time, whatever was explained in between; a test pins it. *Consequences:* nobody reads the number as a hold-out fit.
- **DEC-677 — The OPE coverage tests use 200 seeds and need 87% coverage.** *Context:* `test_ips_and_dr_cover_the_true_policy_value_and_dr_is_narrower` and `test_dr_stays_unbiased_with_a_wrong_outcome_model` asked for at least 26 of 30 seeds inside the 95% interval. At a true coverage of 0.94 a correct estimator misses that 3% of the time per cell: the binomial tail, not the estimator, set the false-alarm rate. *Decision:* 200 fixed seeds and at least 174 hits (87%, no lower than 26/30); the tail is then 7e-5 at a coverage of 0.94 and 3e-6 at 0.95. DR must still be narrower than IPS in every seed. The two tests take about 16 s together. *Consequences:* a genuine loss of coverage still fails; a change of estimator or generator no longer fails by chance.
- **DEC-678 — Uplift API errors use Phase 1's envelope, and their codes are documented in `docs/UPLIFT.md` §17.** *Context:* plan §13.4 asks every user-facing error for a code, a message and a suggestion. The six check findings have all three. The HTTP errors of `api/routes/uplift.py` use Phase 1's M1 envelope `{code, message, path}`, which has no suggestion field, and their codes were written down nowhere. *Decision:* keep the house envelope (Phase 1's own routes do the same, and a second shape would split every client), write each message so it says what to do, and list every code with its status, route, meaning and remedy in `docs/UPLIFT.md` §17. *Consequences:* one error shape across the API; a new uplift error code needs a row there.
- **DEC-679 — Correction to DEC-601's count: five places in `engine/config.py`, two lines per pipeline entry point.** *Context:* DEC-601 says `engine/config.py` gained "exactly four declarations" and each `Pipeline` entry point "one line". *Decision:* the accurate count, as the `CROSS_BRANCH_REQUESTS` announcement has it: five places in `engine/config.py` (the import of `engine.uplift.config` as well as the four declarations) and two lines at the top of `Pipeline.run_train` and `run_score` each (the walrus lookup and its `return`). Nothing else changes. *Consequences:* the DEC and the announcement agree.
- **DEC-680 — A loosened arm floor is recorded in `run.json`, and DEC-607's reason for allowing it is narrowed.** *Context:* DEC-607 keeps `uplift.min_arm_rows` and `min_arm_positives` overridable per run "because a small arm only widens the intervals". The audit ran 1,500 rows with both floors at 1: the run trained and was labelled causal, with 223 and 227 rows in the hold-out arms. That is sound - a small random arm is still an experiment, and `TREATMENT_NOT_RANDOM` still runs - but percentile bootstrap intervals under-cover on very small arms, so "only widens" is optimistic. *Decision:* the overrides stay allowed; the override is recorded in `run.json`'s `overrides`, as every override is, and `docs/UPLIFT.md` §14 now says that on a very small arm the intervals are less reliable than their 95 % claims. Copying the floors a run used into `uplift_evaluation.json` is left as a follow-up, not done here. *Consequences:* the claim in the docs matches what the statistics can promise; a reviewer finds a loosened floor in the run record.

<!-- ---- END PHASE-3B ---- -->
<!-- ---- PLAN-E (pilot) — append only below this line ---- -->

## Plan E — Pilot readiness (DEC-900 … DEC-949)

Plan E (M59–M64) makes the platform ready for a first client pilot: the data request kit and the
pre-flight checker, the data readiness, business results and value reports, the plain-language help
catalogue, demo mode and the feedback loop. It was developed on `main`, the session's designated
branch; the protocol calls the workstream `plan-e-pilot`. It runs beside Plans D and F and reads
artefacts only: no Plan E route trains, scores or changes a check, and no engine stage was edited.
The code is `engine/pilot/`, `api/routes/pilot.py`, `ui/modules/pilot/`, `configs/pilot/`, four
scripts and `docs/pilot/`; the operator's guide is [`pilot/PLAYBOOK.md`](pilot/PLAYBOOK.md).

- **DEC-900 — Plan E's shared-file blocks come after Phase 3b's, it claims DEC-900 … DEC-949, and it owns the pilot paths.** *Context:* `PARALLEL_WORK_PROTOCOL.md` §4 gives each workstream a marked block in every shared file, `tests/unit/test_shared_file_markers.py` pins their order, and Plan E started after Phases 2, 3a, 4a, 4b and 3b had all merged, while Plans D and F run beside it. *Decision:* every shared file gets a `PLAN-E (pilot)` block after its `PHASE-3B` one, and the marker test's `PHASES` ends with `PLAN-E`. The §4 table gains the row `DEC-900…949 | Plan E — pilot readiness (M59–M64)`, which is the claim; the fifty numbers are enough for one plan and leave DEC-950 up unallocated. §3's ownership table gives Plan E `engine/pilot/**`, `api/routes/pilot.py`, `ui/modules/pilot/**`, `configs/pilot/**`, `scripts/preflight.py`, `scripts/gen_data_request.py`, `scripts/seed_demo.py`, `tests/**/pilot/**` and `docs/pilot/**`, and §1 records the workstream as `plan-e-pilot`, developed on `main`. The frozen files are untouched. *Consequences:* "append at the end of your block" still means one place to every reader; a Plan D or F file never needs a Plan E edit, nor a Plan E file a D or F one.
- **DEC-901 — `Settings.demo_mode`, off by default, is the only new setting.** *Context:* a sales or management demo needs the screens to open on a synthetic client, and the protocol allows only added `Settings` fields. *Decision:* `demo_mode: bool = False`, read from `MARKETING_AI_DEMO_MODE` in `ENV_VARS`, and appended to `infra/naming.SETTINGS_FIELDS` in `ENV_VARS` order so `tests/infra/test_settings_contract.py` stays green (the omission DEC-800 had to repair for Phase 4b). The setting seeds nothing: seeding is `scripts/seed_demo.py` (DEC-910). With it on, `GET /pilot/demo` serves the seeded manifest (or says `make demo-seed` when none exists), `GET /pilot/demo/raw/{variant}` serves the raw tables, and each feedback entry is flagged `demo`; with it off both demo routes act as if no demo were seeded (`DEMO_NOT_AVAILABLE` for the tables). `make demo` seeds when needed and serves on :8000 with the variable set. *Consequences:* a deployment that upgrades behaves exactly as before; a demo can be seeded into a deployment's storage and shown only where the flag is on.
- **DEC-902 — The help catalogue explains every *check* code, not every error code, and `configs/pilot/help.yaml` is its one source.** *Context:* plan M60 asks that every warning a client meets be explained in plain words with a fix. The engine and the API carry hundreds of coded errors, but most of them (a missing run, a refused sign-in, a bad request body) are not data problems a client analyst fixes, and they already arrive with a message and a suggestion. *Decision:* `help.yaml`'s `codes` hold a `title`, a one-sentence `meaning` and a `fix` for every code a client's data can raise: `engine.contracts.CHECK_CODES` (Phase 1 and the Phase 2 onboarding table), `UPLIFT_VALIDATION_CODES`, the drift verdicts `DRIFT_WATCH` and `DRIFT_DRIFTED`, `THRESHOLD_FALLBACK` and the pre-flight checker's five `PREFLIGHT_*` codes - 60 entries. `settings` give a `meaning` for every advanced-setting path, a list index written `[*]` (`help.setting_key`) so one entry covers every band; `terms` is the glossary and `metrics` the plain names. Only `engine/pilot/help.py` reads the file; the readiness report, the pre-flight page and the in-app "?" (through `GET /pilot/help`) all ask it. Tests fail when a code the engine can raise or a setting of any use case has no entry, and when an entry names a code nothing raises; an unknown code gets no entry rather than an invented sentence. *Consequences:* a warning reads the same on the client's laptop, in the PDF and beside the pill; a new check code fails the build until someone explains it; engine and HTTP errors keep the wording the API already sends.
- **DEC-903 — One `ReportDocument`, drawn as HTML and as a PDF by fpdf2.** *Context:* every pilot report (pre-flight, readiness, results, value) must exist on screen and as a file a marketing head prints, and two independently written layouts would drift. *Decision:* `engine/pilot/document.py` defines a `ReportDocument` of typed blocks (`Heading`, `Paragraph`, `Bullets`, `KeyValues`, `Table`, `Callout`, `Verdict` with `ready`/`warnings`/`not_ready`/`info`, `Bars`), built once by the module that read the artefacts; `render_html` and `render_pdf` only lay it out and compute nothing. The HTML escapes every string (column and file names are the client's) and carries no script. The PDF is rendered on the server with fpdf2 (pure Python, `fpdf2==2.8.5` in `pyproject.toml`'s PLAN-E block; `requirements-freeze.txt` gains it and its dependency `defusedxml`) using the DejaVu Sans TTFs that matplotlib, already in the freeze through AutoGluon, ships - found with `find_spec`, without importing matplotlib. Where they are absent the core Helvetica font is used and the few characters it cannot draw are spelled out (`₹` → `Rs.`, `—` → `-`, `→` → `->`) rather than the export failing. Logos are named placeholder boxes. Every report route takes `format=html|pdf|json`. *Consequences:* a number cannot appear in the PDF and not on the page; no headless browser or system font is needed; the pre-flight kit imports the model without fpdf2.
- **DEC-904 — The plain-language rule is enforced by a jargon checker, with the terms a report needs explained, not banned.** *Context:* plan E §3: "no model jargon without a one-line explanation". A review by a person stays the rule, but a person misses "AUC" in the ninth callout. *Decision:* `engine/pilot/plain.py` holds `JARGON`, lower-case stems matched at a word start (`feature` catches `features`; a stem ending in `$` matches the whole word, so `target$` leaves "targeted offer" alone), with the screen names the client sees (`features screen`, `mapping screen`, `setup screen`) exempt; `jargon_in` returns what it finds. The help catalogue, the data request and the readiness and results reports are run through it by the tests. Engine phrases that reach a client through config descriptions or check messages are rewritten by `configs/pilot/data_request.yaml`'s `plain_phrases` (longest first: "snapshot date" → "prediction date", "subscriber" → "customer"). Words a report does need - lift, decile, control group, confidence interval - are not on the list; they are in `help.yaml`'s `terms`, and `terms_used` finds which a report used so the results report ends with their explanations. *Consequences:* a sentence with model jargon fails the build instead of reaching a client; the list is a floor, not a substitute for reading the page.
- **DEC-905 — The data request and its templates are generated from the configs the engine runs on.** *Context:* the first document of a pilot tells a client's analyst which tables, columns and history to export; written by hand it would ask for less than a build needs or for columns no feature reads. *Decision:* `engine/pilot/data_request.py` builds it from the use cases, `configs/roles.yaml` and `configs/pilot/data_request.yaml` (the wording only). The customer table is always requested, the outcome's table (`label.role`) is required, a table a suggested feature reads is recommended; a column is required when a build needs it, needed when a feature or the label reads it, useful otherwise. History: the minimum is `onboarding.snapshots.min_history_days + label.horizon_days`, the sum below which a build fails `TOO_LITTLE_HISTORY`; the recommendation is the longer of that minimum history and the longest feature window, plus the horizon, plus `max_snapshots - 1` snapshot periods. The campaign table win-back needs to be measured against a control group comes from `extra_tables` in the wording file, keyed by use-case id, because `tests/unit/test_no_use_case_branching.py` forbids `engine/` from naming one. The role templates are header-only CSVs written the way `engine.templates` writes a use case's: a role has no example rows to show, so none are invented. `scripts/gen_data_request.py` writes `docs/pilot/DATA_REQUEST.md` and `docs/pilot/templates/*_template.csv`; `--check` (`make pilot-check`) fails when they are stale. *Consequences:* a new feature or a longer window changes the request by regenerating it; the committed request is drift-checked like the API docs.
- **DEC-906 — The pre-flight checker reuses the platform's own ingest, profiling and onboarding checks, on the client's laptop.** *Context:* plan M59 wants a client to find a broken extract before it leaves their systems, and a second implementation of the checks would disagree with the upload's. *Decision:* `engine/pilot/preflight.py` reads each file with `engine.stages.ingest.read_upload`, profiles it with `engine.onboarding.sources.profile_source`, and runs the onboarding checks that need only the identified customer ID and event date (`ONBOARDING_CHECKS_RUN`: `ENTITY_DUPLICATE_KEYS`, `JOIN_KEY_COVERAGE_LOW`, `KEY_FORMAT_MISMATCH`, `EVENT_TIME_UNPARSEABLE`, `DATE_FORMAT_AMBIGUOUS` and four more) from `engine.onboarding.validate.CHECK_REGISTRY`, keeping their codes. Only what a checker without a mapping screen can meet gets a code of its own: `PREFLIGHT_FILE_UNREADABLE`, `PREFLIGHT_FILE_SKIPPED`, `PREFLIGHT_TABLE_NOT_RECOGNISED`, `PREFLIGHT_TABLE_MISSING` (an error for a required table, a warning otherwise) and `PREFLIGHT_KEY_NOT_FOUND`; it says it cannot tell rather than guess. `DATE_FORMAT_AMBIGUOUS` is a warning before the upload, because it is answered by naming the format on the mapping screen. A customer table below `validation.min_rows` is a warning, not an error: the engine applies that floor to the built dataset, one row per customer per prediction date, which may still clear it. A file the profiler cannot handle becomes `PREFLIGHT_FILE_UNREADABLE`, never a crash. Only counts, names and date ranges reach the page. `python -m scripts.preflight <folder>` writes `preflight_report.html` and exits 0, 1 when a problem blocks the files, 2 on wrong usage. Heavy libraries are imported lazily and a test runs the checker in a fresh interpreter and fails if it imported any of fourteen heavy libraries (AutoGluon, scikit-learn, LightGBM, torch, SHAP, FastAPI, DuckDB, boto3, fpdf2, matplotlib among them). `scripts/build_pilot_kit.py` (`make pilot-kit`) zips `engine/`, `configs/`, the checker, the data request, its templates, a README and a `requirements-preflight.txt` pinning pandas, pyarrow, pydantic and PyYAML into `dist/pilot-kit.zip`. *Consequences:* the pre-flight page and the readiness report after the upload speak one vocabulary; the kit holds no data, model or credential and needs no network.
- **DEC-907 — The readiness report's verdict is the build's own, explained and never overridden.** *Context:* plan M60's traffic light could be tempted to soften a failed build or harden a warning; plan E §3 forbids changing a check. *Decision:* `engine/pilot/readiness.py` reads only what the build and the upload recorded: `build_status.json`, `build_report.json`, `dataset_manifest.json`, the recipe, mappings and sources in the client store, and each source's `profile.json`. The verdict is *Not ready* exactly when `BuildReport.passed` is false, the build failed or it finished without a report; a build still pending or running gets a neutral *Still building*; an artefact that does not validate is treated as missing, so the page degrades instead of failing; *Ready with warnings* when it passed with warnings; *Ready* otherwise. Each finding is a callout: the catalogue's title, meaning and fix (DEC-902) beside the engine's own message and suggestion passed through `plain_phrases` (DEC-904), with an acknowledged error shown as a warning that "does not block the pilot" because the build treated it so. `GET /pilot/readiness/{dataset_id}` is a Viewer read. *Consequences:* the report cannot say Ready about a dataset the platform would refuse to train on, or the reverse; changing a verdict means changing a check, which Plan E does not do.
- **DEC-908 — The value view reads a measured effect from either of two sources and prices it only as a range.** *Context:* plan M62 wants a campaign's value in rupees. The platform already measures a campaign twice: Phase 3b's `POST /runs/{id}/campaign-results` writes `incrementality_report.json` with a 95% interval, and Phase 4b's `POST /runs/{id}/outcomes` writes `incrementality_input.json` with the arms' counts and no interval. *Decision:* `engine/pilot/roi.py::compute_roi` reads a mature report when it exists; otherwise, for a binary outcome, the ingestion counts (so counts ingested after an early, immature `campaign-results` call are not hidden behind it), with the interval from the same `engine.uplift.incrementality.newcombe_interval` Phase 3b uses scaled by the treated rows, so the two sources agree when both exist. It computes no effect of its own. The client's figures (`RoiInputs`: value of one extra outcome, offer cost when taken, contact cost, `outcome_is_good`) are stored at `runs/<run_id>/pilot_roi_inputs.json` through `PUT /pilot/roi/{run_id}` (Analyst) and printed on the report. `outcome_is_good: false` (churn: fewer leavers is the gain) negates the effect and swaps its ends; when the client has not said, the default comes from `configs/pilot/value.yaml` - false when the campaign is measured on the very target or label of a use case listed there as an outcome to prevent (churn, late payment, default, fault, late delivery) - so a churn campaign is never reported backwards before anybody enters a value; `entered_by` is always the signed-in user the audit row names, never a name from the request body; the offer is counted as taken by the treated customers who had the outcome when it is good and by those who did not when it is not; contacts are counted on every treated row. Every amount is a `Money` range from the interval's low end to its high end with the estimate between, formatted in Indian grouping by `format_inr`, and the verdict says whether the range is above zero, below it or straddles it. An immature report shows its `results_available_on`; with no measurement at all the run's outcome window gives the date outcomes can first be measured. *Consequences:* a rupee figure is never a single number and never more certain than the measurement under it; the value depends on the client's inputs and the page says so.
- **DEC-909 — The results report is headlined by first-decile capture, compares with the yardstick model only on the primary measure, and shows anonymous examples.** *Context:* plan M61 asks "how good is it?" in business terms, from artefacts already written, and nothing fabricated may reach a screen (Phase 1 rule 3). *Decision:* `engine/pilot/results.py` reads the champion's training run (`run_config.json`, `evaluation.json`, `decile_lift.json`, `baseline.json`, `feature_importance.json`, the split) and the latest scoring run by that model (`scoring_summary.json`, `row_explanations.parquet`), plus `uplift_evaluation.json` and `segments.json` for an uplift champion. The headline is "the 10% of customers the model flags first contain X% of everyone who …", from the test split's first decile, beside the 10% a random pick would contain; for an uplift champion it is the gain in the top 10% against a random 10%. The yardstick model appears only as a value on the primary measure: `baseline.json` holds no deciles, so there is no yardstick capture line to draw, and none is recomputed. Examples are the top-scored customer of each band (for uplift, the top three by predicted gain), shown as Customer A/B/C with no ID, and free-text values pass through `engine.pii.redact_text`; they come from the latest scoring run, else the training run's test split. A section whose artefact is missing says so. `GET /pilot/results` takes `use_case` (its champion) or `model_id`. *Consequences:* every figure in the report is one an artefact holds; no customer can be identified from it.
- **DEC-910 — The demo is seeded ahead of time, through the platform's own API, from the test generators.** *Context:* plan M63: a demo must never train live or touch a real file, and pictures of screens would show nothing the engine made. *Decision:* `scripts/seed_demo.py` (`make demo-seed`, a few minutes) drives the API in-process with FastAPI's `TestClient`, so it needs a checkout with the dev extra. It generates 2,000 synthetic customers with `tests/fixtures/raw/make_raw.py`, uploads and maps them as client "Demo Telecom" - making the one correction a person would make, setting the complaints' event date back to `TICKET_DT` (and resolution to `RESOLVED_DT`) where the suggester, flagging `MAPPING_LOW_CONFIDENCE`, had swapped them - builds a dataset, and trains a churn champion with one tree family (strategy `fast`, `LightGBM` only, no ensemble, two minutes) so every customer's reasons come from the exact tree explainer. It scores next month through a recipe replay with a control group, and measures that campaign on outcomes simulated with a planted effect uploaded through `POST /runs/{id}/outcomes`, because the periodic dataset's composite key is refused by `POST /runs/{id}/campaign-results` (`COMPOSITE_KEY_NOT_SUPPORTED`). A win-back uplift model is trained on `tests/fixtures/make_uplift_data.py`'s randomised campaign (`base_model: lightgbm`, 50 bootstrap samples), scored and measured through `campaign-results`. The raw tables, clean and with one planted `ENTITY_DUPLICATE_KEYS` problem, are kept under `pilot/demo/raw/<variant>/` for the pre-flight exercise, and `DemoManifest` is written at `pilot/demo/demo.json`. Criteo is excluded (non-commercial licence, Plan D R2): `EXCLUDED_DATA` is checked against the manifest's `data_sources` by the seeder and by a test. *Consequences:* every demo screen shows engine artefacts; nothing trains while anybody watches; `engine/pilot/demo.py` imports no test code, so the API reads a demo a checkout seeded.
- **DEC-911 — Feedback is stored in platform storage, redacted, audited and exported by an Admin only.** *Context:* plan M64 puts a feedback button on every screen; what a person types can contain a phone number, and the audit trail may carry no data values (DEC-705). *Decision:* `POST /pilot/feedback` (Viewer) stores one `FeedbackEntry` per file under `pilot/feedback/<id>.json` in the platform's storage, so it works on a laptop and on S3 alike. The screen must be a route (hash, slashes, ids; no query string, else 422 `FEEDBACK_INVALID`); the text is cut to 1,000 characters and passed through `engine.pii.redact_text`, and the entry lists the kinds masked. The write is audited by the access middleware like every write, the route setting only the entry's id and content hash. `GET /pilot/feedback/export` is Admin with `audit_reads`, as CSV or JSONL; the CSV prefixes with a quote any cell starting `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return, as the audit export does. *Consequences:* no new table or schema revision; feedback given during a demo is flagged `demo`; an export is itself in the audit trail.
- **DEC-912 — The pilot UI adds to other workstreams' screens without editing them.** *Context:* the tooltips, the feedback button and the tour must appear on screens owned by Phase 1, 2, 3b and 4b, which Plan E may not edit. *Decision:* `ui/modules/pilot/index.js` is loaded by its own `<script type="module">` in `index.html`'s PLAN-E block (it imports `router.js` for `registerModule`, so an import back from the router would run it too early), registers `#/pilot`, `#/pilot/view/readiness/<id>`, `#/pilot/view/results/<uc>` and `#/pilot/value/<run>`, and inserts a thin bar above `#app` (a Demo Telecom badge in demo mode, a link to the Pilot screen, "Take the tour") and a fixed feedback button outside `#app`, so no repaint removes either; the button sends the route, never a value from the page. `help.js` watches `#app` with a `MutationObserver` and puts a "?" beside every `.pill` whose text is a catalogue code and every `[data-path]` settings control the catalogue explains, opening one shared popover; nothing is invented for an unknown code. The tour has six steps (overview, setup, results, output, campaign results, the pilot's reports), opens the demo's own screens when one is seeded, and remembers it was seen in `localStorage` only. Reports are fetched through the wrapped `fetch` (so a sign-in is carried, DEC-790) and shown in a `sandbox`ed iframe's `srcdoc`, where no script runs. If `GET /pilot/demo` or `GET /pilot/help` fails, the page works without the badge or the buttons. *Consequences:* no other workstream's file changed; the tooltips follow new pills and settings automatically; a forgotten tour is a convenience lost, not a record.

<!-- ---- END PLAN-E ---- -->
- **DEC-200 — Everything generative lives under one `generative:` block, in `engine.yaml:defaults` and in a use-case file alike.** *Context:* the Phase 3a plan §4.1 sketches `generative:` in `engine.yaml` with `rca:` and `copy:` inside it, and §4.2 then writes `rca:` and `copy:` at the *top level* of a use-case file. Both spellings cannot be right: `deep_merge` merges the defaults under a use case path by path, so a top-level `rca:` in a use-case file has no default to merge with, and `extra="forbid"` rejects whichever of the two a reader guessed wrong. *Decision:* one block, `generative.{kind,llm,budget,rag,knowledge_base,reference_set,rca,campaign_copy}`, identical in shape wherever it appears. The plan's `evaluation:` sub-block is called `reference_set` because `UseCaseConfig.evaluation` already means the model's evaluation and one word cannot carry both meanings without a reader having to ask which. `kind: none` is the default, which is what makes the block inert for the five predictive use cases rather than something they must each switch off. *Consequences:* one merge path, one place to look, and `tests/unit/test_use_case_files_minimal.py` keeps working unchanged — every `generative.*` leaf a use-case file writes has a default at the same dotted path and must differ from it, which is why `kind`'s default is the off value.
- **DEC-201 — The copy block is spelled `campaign_copy`, in the YAML as well as in the model.** *Context:* the plan calls it `copy:`. A pydantic field named `copy` shadows `BaseModel.copy`, which pydantic reports as a `UserWarning` at class-construction time — a warning printed on every import of `engine.config`, in a repository whose lint gate is `mypy --strict` and whose test run is quiet. *Decision:* spell it in full in both places, so the YAML key and the field name stay the same word. *Consequences:* one deliberate divergence from the plan's spelling, recorded here and in the block's own docstring; nothing in the codebase has to remember an alias.
- **DEC-202 — A prompt is a versioned file with front matter and two named sections, not a string in code.** *Context:* plan §2.1 principle 7 asks for prompts as config with a version and a hash in the artefact, and says no more. A prompt still has to carry a system message and a user message, and a renderer still has to know which variables it needs. *Decision:* `configs/prompts/<name>.v<N>.md`: YAML front matter naming the prompt, its version, its purpose and the variables it expects, then a `# System` section and a `# User` section. The loader renders each section through a **sandboxed** jinja2 environment with `StrictUndefined`, so a missing variable is an error at render time rather than a prompt with a hole in it, and the declared `variables` list is checked against what the caller supplied. *Consequences:* a wording change is a diff a reviewer can read; the hash in the artefact is the hash of the file, so an artefact names exactly the prompt that produced it; and a prompt cannot quietly start depending on a variable nobody passes.
- **DEC-203 — `LLMClient` is a protocol with two implementations, and the fake is shipped, not test-only.** *Context:* plan §2.1 principle 9 wants the fast suite to run on a fake. The tempting shape is a fake that lives under `tests/`. *Decision:* both implementations live in `engine/llm.py`, and `generative.llm.backend` defaults to `fake`. *Consequences:* a developer with no AWS credentials can open every screen, build an index and generate copy, and see the guardrails and the budget work — the parts most likely to be wrong — without spending anything; the fake is held to the same lint and type bar as the real client; and the default is the one that cannot cost money by accident.
- **DEC-204 — A model id is configuration, never a literal in code, and `bedrock` without one is a configuration error.** *Context:* model ids change, differ by region and are the sort of string that ends up copied into three modules. *Decision:* `generative.llm.{generation,judge,embedding}_model_id` are the only places one appears; they default to `""`; and `LlmConfig` refuses a document whose backend is `bedrock` while any of them is blank, naming the first one missing. *Consequences:* switching a deployment to a new model is one file; the failure for a half-configured deployment happens when the configuration is read rather than three stages into a run; and `tests/integration/test_ui.py`'s ban on vendor names in the UI stays satisfiable, because the id reaches the screen from an artefact rather than from a constant.
- **DEC-205 — Human review is on for campaign copy and off for root-cause summaries.** *Context:* the plan's own open-decisions table leaves this to a manager, with those defaults assumed. *Decision:* keep them, and make the reason explicit in the config: copy is written to be sent to a customer, so nothing leaves the queue without a person; a root-cause summary is written to be read by the team that asked for it, and gating it behind an approval would only delay the person who requested it. *Consequences:* `generative.campaign_copy.require_human_review` is `true` and `generative.rca.require_human_review` is `false`; both are config, so a client that wants the other answer changes one line rather than asking for code.
- **DEC-206 — `tests/fixtures/make_docs.py` is type-checked with the source tree, exactly as `make_data.py` is.** *Context:* DEC-041 put the synthetic data generator inside `mypy`'s `files` because every later milestone builds on it. The knowledge-base generator is in the same position for the generative milestones. *Decision:* add it to the same list. *Consequences:* one more file under `--strict`; a fixture that every generative test leans on cannot drift into `Any`.
- **DEC-207 — The PDF fixture writer is ours, not a new dependency.** *Context:* the generative tests need PDFs with real text in them, and `pypdf` reads PDFs rather than writing them. The alternatives were a PDF-writing library added to the project for a fixture, or committed binary PDFs. *Decision:* `make_docs.write_pdf` emits an uncompressed PDF with one base-14 font — about sixty lines of page description — and `tests/fixtures/test_make_docs.py` proves `pypdf` reads back every sentence that went in. *Consequences:* no production-adjacent dependency is bought for a test fixture, the fixture is a diffable text-shaped file rather than an opaque binary, and the one thing the hand-written writer can get wrong (an em dash the WinAnsi encoding cannot carry) is the thing the tests check for by name.
- **DEC-208 — `configs/llm_prices.yaml` ships empty, and an unknown price is `null` and an em dash rather than a guess.** *Context:* plan §6 wants a dated price table and says "never guess". Prices differ by region and by contract, so any number shipped here would be a number on a screen that nobody measured — the thing plan §13.3 forbids as squarely as a made-up accuracy would be. *Decision:* the file ships with its shape, an `as_of` of `null` and no rows. Calls, tokens and latency are still metered exactly; `cost_estimate_usd` is `null`, a `PRICE_UNKNOWN` warning names the model that was missing, and the screen shows the em dash it already shows for any figure it does not have (plan §2.1, principle 5). `budget.max_calls_per_run` still bounds such a run; `max_cost_usd_per_run` cannot, and the run records that it could not. *Consequences:* an operator gets a true "we did not price this" instead of a plausible fiction, and filling the table in is a documented one-file change that turns the cost line on.
- **DEC-209 — The advanced-settings sweeps are narrowed to the use cases that train something, and the planned-entry rules move to a fixture.** *Context:* two shipped test sweeps assumed things that adding the first generative use case made false. `advanced_settings_schema` already returned no stages for `ai_type: generative` — correctly, since none of its eight stages is a choice a RAG assistant has — so seven sweeps over every use case failed on the eighth by it being exactly what it is. And `tests/unit/test_industry_config.py` proved the planned-entry rules against the one planned entry the shipped telecom file had, which Phase 3a turns into an available one. *Decision:* the settings sweep is computed off the configs (`ai_type is not GENERATIVE`), never listed by hand, and is paired with a test that a narrowed sweep still covers something and one that a generative use case really does get an empty table; the planned-entry rules are proved against a new valid fixture, `industry_with_a_planned_use_case.yaml`, so they outlive whichever use cases happen to be unbuilt. *Consequences:* the rules both tests existed to protect are still enforced, neither test needs editing when a use case is added by YAML alone (plan §2.1, principle 1), and neither passes vacuously.
- **DEC-210 — Generative artefacts get their own registry map, parallel to `ARTEFACT_REGISTRY` rather than inside it.** *Context:* `tests/unit/test_artefact_registry.py` pins `ARTEFACT_REGISTRY` name-for-name and asserts that every addition beyond plan §7 is a deliberate, commented entry in a second list; `TRAIN_ARTEFACTS` and `SCORE_ARTEFACTS` have their intersection pinned too, and the flow tests assert that every member of each was really written. A generative artefact belongs to none of those sets: `root_cause_summary.json` is not written by a run, it is written *over* one, and a scoring run that nobody asked for copy on is complete without `copy_batch.json`. *Decision:* `engine.generative.contracts.GENERATIVE_ARTEFACTS` and `GENERATIVE_TABULAR_SCHEMAS` are parallel maps, proved disjoint from the predictive ones by a test, and the artefact route serves the union. `ARTEFACT_REGISTRY` is untouched. *Consequences:* the predictive registry goes on meaning "what a run writes", which is what its tests are about; a generative artefact is served by the same URL with no special case; and the two can be told apart by which map a filename is in, which is what the `GET /runs/{id}` body needs in order to show a generative block only when there is one.
- **DEC-211 — A generative job keeps its own status document rather than appending to the run's.** *Context:* a root-cause or copy job runs over a run that has already finished. `status.json` is that run's record of how it went, with every stage `done` and a final state. *Decision:* `root_cause_status.json`, `copy_status.json` and `index_status.json` are separate documents sharing one `GenerativeStatus` model, whose stage keys are plain strings rather than `StageKey` members — a root-cause job has one step per segment and their names are data (`generate:High`), where the predictive stage vocabulary is closed and pinned by `engine.pipeline`. The state vocabulary is `RunState`, unchanged, so one screen renders either. *Consequences:* a finished run's documents are never rewritten, which is what makes them evidence; `StageKey`, `TRAIN_STAGES` and `SCORE_STAGES` do not move; and a generative job that fails leaves a status a person can read without any doubt about which run state it describes.
- **DEC-212 — The generative package has its own coded error table, and `generative_error` is the only way it raises.** *Context:* plan §13.4 wants every user-facing failure to carry a code, a message and a suggestion, and `ENGINE_ERRORS` and `SCORE_ERRORS` already do exactly that. *Decision:* `GENERATIVE_ERRORS` is a third table in the same shape; every code is a module constant; `generative_error(code, **values)` fills the template, so a code nobody wrote down raises `KeyError` at the raise site rather than reaching a user. *Consequences:* the codes are enumerable, mappable onto HTTP statuses and assertable; a new failure mode has to be described before it can happen; and `engine.errors.run_error` turns one of these into a `RunError` with no edit to that module, because it reads `code` and `message` structurally.
- **DEC-213 — The `name` PII detector is not run over free text.** *Context:* `engine.stages.ingest.PII_DETECTORS` is reused verbatim for the in-text redaction the generative engine needs, because two copies of a pattern drift. One detector does not survive the change of venue: `name` matches "one to four capitalised words", which over a column with an open vocabulary distinguishes a roster of people from a category, and inside a sentence matches the first word of every sentence, every product and every place. *Decision:* the other four run; `name` is left out, and the omission is written down where the scanner is built rather than inferred from its absence. *Consequences:* a complaint keeps its meaning after redaction instead of becoming a row of markers; a personal name genuinely written in a complaint can survive redaction, which is a real gap and is the reason complaint text reaches a prompt as a sample rather than in bulk; and nobody adds `name` back without reading the paragraph that says why it went.
- **DEC-214 — Phase 3a adds a second fake beside `FakeLLMClient` rather than changing it.** *Context:* DEC-076 built the shared fake around a digest, deliberately: visibly machine-made, reaching nothing, assertable byte for byte. That is right for a seam and wrong for retrieval — a hash embedding has no semantics, so the chunk that answers a question sits no closer to it than any other, and `[fake completion 3f2a…]` quotes no extract and cites no chunk. A RAG test against it would assert that one random number beat another, and every grounding test would pass for the wrong reason. *Decision:* `FakeLLMClient` is untouched and its tests still pin it. `GroundedFakeLLMClient` implements the same protocol, hashes words into buckets so shared vocabulary means proximity, and builds its reply out of the extracts, the evidence pack or the allowed placeholder list it was handed; `GroundedFakeMode` then breaks exactly one rule at a time. `build_client` hands the grounded one to a generative flow. *Consequences:* two fakes, which is one more than ideal and is the price of not weakening the shared one; the whole retrieval path is genuinely exercised offline; and plan §13.3 applies to both — with a fake backend, no path that renders generated text may be reachable.
- **DEC-215 — An embedding batch's tokens are estimated, and the artefact says so.** *Context:* the `LLMClient` protocol's `embed` returns vectors and nothing else, because no provider reports usage through it, and DEC-076 chose three methods and no more on purpose. The meter still has to charge a budget for the call. *Decision:* the tokens are worked out from the text with the same `APPROX_CHARS_PER_TOKEN` ratio the chunker sizes a chunk with and both fakes answer `count_tokens` with — one definition, so three parts of the system that have to agree cannot drift — and `LlmUsageReport.warnings` carries `TOKENS_ESTIMATED:embedding`. *Consequences:* an embedding's cost is good enough to budget with and is not presented as a measurement; the alternative, a `count_tokens` round trip per text, would have cost a real API call per chunk to improve a number nobody bills from.
- **DEC-216 — PII found in a knowledge document is a warning; PII found in complaint text is redacted.** *Context:* the same detectors run over both, and the obvious consistency — redact everywhere — is wrong in one of the two places. A knowledge document is the client's own published material, and the support address, escalation number or sample account format printed in it is frequently the very thing a question is about; redacting it damages the answer and leaves the assistant citing `[REDACTED:email]` as the address to write to. Complaint text is a customer's own words, was never published, and reaches a prompt only as evidence. *Decision:* `build_index` records `PII_IN_DOCS:<kinds>` on the manifest, names the kinds and never a value, and indexes the text exactly as written; `redaction.redact` is applied to every complaint before it is put in an evidence pack. *Consequences:* an operator uploading a document that genuinely should not have been published is told, in the artefact, which kinds were seen and in which document, and can withdraw it — the engine does not decide that for them; a customer's words never reach a model unredacted; and the asymmetry is written down here rather than read as an oversight in whichever module is looked at second.
- **DEC-217 — A chunk is embedded with its document and heading in front of it, not bare.** *Context:* heading-aware chunking already guarantees `Chunk.section` is a fact rather than a guess, and that fact was being thrown away at the one moment it is worth most. The words a question uses are very often in the heading and nowhere in the prose beneath it: "Late payment and reconnection" is the entire vocabulary of "what is the late payment fee?", while the paragraph under it says "a fee of ₹100 or 2% of the outstanding amount, whichever is higher" and never repeats the heading at all. *Decision:* `chunking.embedding_text` renders a chunk as `"<doc id> <section>\n\n<text>"` and `build_index` embeds that; `Chunk.text` is untouched, so a citation still quotes the document's words and the prompt still receives the passage. The question is embedded bare — the prefix is context the passage lacks, not a format both sides must share. `doc_id` is used rather than `document` because a filename carries an extension, and "pdf" is a word in every chunk of every PDF. *Consequences:* measured over the 45 answerable questions of the reference set, the right document is retrieved 40 times where the bare passage managed 28, and the margin between a real question and an off-topic one *widens* rather than narrowing, because a heading is specific where a body paragraph is discursive; an index built before this change embeds differently from one built after, so the change is a rebuild and `ChunkConfig` records the settings that produced each index.
- **DEC-218 — The similarity floor is calibrated per embedding model, and the shipped default is calibrated for a real one.** *Context:* `min_similarity: 0.25` decides whether the assistant refuses without calling a model, so it is the single most consequential number in the RAG config — and it is not portable.
